# Current manual build entry

Run extras/build.cmd from the version root, or run build.cmd inside extras.
It invokes the existing build_all.py with unbuffered console output, keeps
TEMP/TMP inside extras/build/temp, prevents concurrent manual builds, preserves
the build exit code and pauses for inspection on success or failure.
Python remains an explicit dependency of this version. The underlying build
logic and core sources are unchanged. See ../etc/build-entry-location.json.

# Current acceptance snapshot — 2026-11-12

All three core artifacts now match the frozen 7.0a targets byte for byte.
See [aggregate evidence](../etc/three-core-exact-20261112.json).
IO.SYS: 223148 bytes; MSDOS.SYS: 6 bytes; COMMAND.COM: 92870 bytes.
COMMAND has 35 linked modules: 34 modified from baseline and 1 unchanged.
This continuation changed one source file, bios/sysconf.asm: declare the
260-byte parser buffer explicitly before inclusion, as COMMAND already does.
The BIOS and loader rebuilt exactly; the fresh kernel and configuration builds
were integrated into a complete exact IO.SYS. Shared COMMAND inputs remain
identical to its exact-build stage. No VM acceptance is required.

Binary reconstruction and project delivery are verified. The full-build entry point, dependency inventory, file-ledger reconciliation and final audit are complete. The pinned CRT and registered non-executable logo remain declared
build dependencies; original Microsoft source-text identity is not claimed.
Entries below are chronological research history, not the latest acceptance state.

# Current state

The existing SHELL parser linked with DOS7 command buffers and getchr matches all 99 target bytes; query_user and conflp were placeholders in this test. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-030-shell-parser-linked.json).


File 33 contains a DOS7 startup record rotation helper and two observed boot flag bytes. The linked 72-byte helper and 703-byte command data match target; callers, flag semantics and the third copy source at runtime remain unresolved. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-033-shell-rotation.json).


The DOS7 command failure selector now uses the actual WIN.COM and COMMAND.COM table entries and command buffers; the isolated link matches the 34-byte selector, 703-byte buffers and 75-byte badfil span. Surrounding fallback and flag writers remain incomplete. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-032-command-error-selection.json).


DOS7 command path/tail buffers now reconstruct the target 703-byte layout; the legacy fallback consumers are not yet adapted and are not a completed execution path. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-033-command-buffers.json).


The DOS7 badfil/error output path uses target message indices 8 and 5; the linked badfil and print span matches all 75 target bytes. Its command-interpreter caller remains unresolved. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-033-badfil.json).


File 34 now uses target indexed message 8021h for compression-driver version failure; its 11-byte branch matches in an isolated link and baddblspace is no longer required in DOS7. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-034-compression-error.json).


The DOS7 interactive prompt path no longer imports the legacy $AutoPrmpt and resolves its common prompt via the target table; an isolated linked 95-byte span matches target. Input logic and all callers remain unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-033-query-prompt.json).


DOS7 error_line plus showlinenum now matches all 64 target bytes in an isolated ML6 link. MASM5 uses a different CMP encoding; full SYSINIT remains unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-030-error-line-showlinenum.json).


The DOS7 error_line path now uses indexed message 8011h and matches all 18 target bytes in an isolated link; showlinenum remains a placeholder in that test. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-030-error-line.json).


DOS7 sysconf no longer emits the unused incorrect_order routine or references badorder; getchr now uses target CS-relative fields and matches 32 target bytes after four address substitutions. Full error handling remains unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-030-getchr-order.json).


File 33 defines the DOS7 ONOFF string used by NUMLOCK. Six data bytes match target; the 39-byte routine matches after two address substitutions. Its final source placement remains unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-033-onoff-data.json).


The legacy YES and NO byte symbols now point to Y and N in target message record 23; the 4299-byte table remains unchanged. Five legacy-only symbols still need consumer reconstruction. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-031-yes-no-alias.json).


DOS7 sysimes now uses the 4299-byte target message table in place of the duplicate legacy block. Twenty-three exact legacy aliases resolve; seven legacy-only names are intentionally not exported pending consumer reconstruction. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-031-message-aliases.json).


An isolated target-layout link joins source-extracted stack allocation, stackinit, allocator and actual message resource; all 5401 compared bytes match after relocation. ORG and diagnostic provider definitions remain; this is not a complete IO.SYS. [Evidence](../etc/stack-chain-linked-evidence.json).


File 32 stack initialization and vector handling now match all 850 target bytes in an isolated historical link after simulated relocation. The test uses diagnostic target layout; full SYSINIT and IO.SYS remain unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-032-stackinit-linked.json).


File 32 restores DOS7 stack initialization and vector-save logic. The 850-byte object span has 761 matching bytes and 89 differing zero object bytes; actual linking is required before treating those as resolved fixups. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-032-stackinit-integration.json).


File 32 now calls the DOS7 marked allocator, appends the stack message and fills the target Windows instance fields. The 137-byte target range matches after 25 manual fixups. Full link, startup policy and stackinit remain unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-032-stack-allocation-integrated.json).


The DOS7 STACKSHIGH command constant, table entry and parser flag are restored. The 20-byte table sequence matches exactly, and the 36-byte parser sequence matches after four target fixups. Other flag users and the allocator caller remain pending. [Evidence](../etc/stackshigh-command-integration.json).


File 30 now contains the DOS7 marked-block allocator with UMB and conventional paths. Its 63 bytes match after twelve manual address and call fixups; the stack initialization caller is not yet connected. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-030-marked-allocation.json).


File 30 now contains the DOS7 low-memory limit check, linked in a diagnostic to the restored indexed printer and real message table. Its 24 bytes match after four target fixups; the allocator call and final link remain pending. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-030-low-memory-check.json).


File 33 now contains the DOS7 indexed-message print routine. An isolated link to the real table matches its 31 target bytes after target address substitution and resolves index 14 to the target 124-byte message. Callers and final layout remain pending. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-033-indexed-print.json).


File 31 includes the reconstructed DOS7 message table and exposes the SYSINIT message symbols. An isolated link resolves a caller and contains the exact 4299-byte target table. Legacy MSBIO.CL3 remains present pending consumer reconciliation. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-031-message-table-integration.json).


A new language-specific SYSINIT message-table source reconstructs 65 offsets and 64 records; 4299 standalone bytes match the target. It is not yet connected to the build, and legacy symbol mapping and message-source generation remain open. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-sysinit-message-table.json).


The memory-limit error path uses inline message token 800Eh and index 14, resolving to the configuration-too-large message. Message 0011 now includes the target NUL; its generated 124 bytes match. The table and dispatcher remain unresolved. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-dosmsg-memory-error-integration.json).


The allocation marker leaf was moved from its provisional location in sysinit1.asm to sysconf.asm, alongside the target allocator’s matching UMB helpers. The 52-byte target match and both legacy object checks pass. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-030-devmark-owner.json).


File 32 now contains the DOS7 allocation marker leaf, whose 52 bytes match the target. Its caller, source ownership, placement and full integration remain unresolved. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-032-devmark-leaf.json).


File 30 now uses the target 256-byte default stack size under DOS7_CONFIG. The reset sequence matches 21 target bytes after substituting three verified field offsets; the legacy object remains unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-030-stack-default-size.json).


File 32 now aliases the DOS7 stack configuration fields to the verified stack header, preserving the 539-byte target match. The separate sysconf default-size path remains unmodified and requires 256-byte DOS7 handling. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-032-shared-stack-state.json).


The DOS7 NUL-terminated stack overflow message 0017 is now restored in the project message source; the historical generator and assembled 144-byte output match target. The message table and its SYSINIT symbol bindings remain unresolved. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-dosmsg-stack-message-integration.json).


File 32 now includes DOS7 stack-message sizing and code/message copy loops. Their 41 bytes match the target after manually substituting three target symbol offsets. The surrounding allocator, instance data and the two new external message-table symbols remain unresolved; this is partial integration only. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-032-stack-copy-integration.json).


File 32 `bios/sysinit1.asm` now contains the partially reconstructed DOS7 hardware interrupt stack unit. The extracted linked 539-byte unit matches the target after simulated relocation; the full legacy MASM object is unchanged and the DOS7 source assembles. Stack-copy initialization, the appended message and full SYSINIT integration remain unresolved; the file is waiting for dependencies. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-032-interrupt-unit-integration.json).


**Current execution rule:** dependency-first topological order, as directed by the user. Core source edits remain paused for files with unresolved dependencies. Sixty-seven source files have been marked reference-qualified after dependency and historical identity checks; their target contributions remain unverified. The modified DOS7_CONFIG msload loader is component-verified against the 1,024-byte target loader range. The modified versiona.inc version, revision, and HMA constants are verified against target code, while its ROM-only flag remains unverified. The modified ds_vers.inc preloader revision is verified against the target initial field and its compare/store references. The modified USA copyright data matches four ranges in target IO.SYS and COMMAND.COM; other languages remain unverified. Complete binary integration remains unverified. The user-authorized `dossym`/`dosmac`/`buffer`/`error` cycle unit passed isolated definition-context assembly and historical identity screening; deferred macro expansion references remain caller dependencies. File 9 `bios/msbio1.asm` is waiting for dependencies, not complete. `lang/usa/messages/usa-ms-command.msg` is waiting for `cmd/command/tranmsg.asm` because the target version message differs from the existing substitution contract. `lang/usa/messages/usa-ms-dos.msg` is paused waiting for `bios/sysinit1.asm` after discovering a stack-message termination dependency. Its 21 modified and three unchanged target-matched strings remain partial evidence, not a completed catalog. The [lexical scan](../etc/file-dependency-scan.json) is preliminary: 220 files, 53 candidate leaves, 5 candidate cycles, and 73 files with unresolved or ambiguous references. These numbers are not validated topology or completed-file counts. The following earlier active-file statements are historical and superseded by this paragraph.


M0 baseline established: the 220-file source tree was materialized from v6.22. Files 1–8 have exact historical reference matches, not proof of 7.0a target equivalence. The project has not established byte-identical 7.0a core binaries.

The earlier status lines incorrectly identified files 10 and 13 as active after work had advanced to file 30. Work has returned to file 9, `bios/msbio1.asm`, to respect the requested order. File 9 is in progress; files 10–30 contain prior screening or partial work and remain pending integration, not certified complete. Of the first 30 ledger entries, 9 are modified and 21 unchanged from v6.22. See the [ledger](../etc/file-ledger.json).

The file-9 diagnostic uses reference objects, synthetic gaps and stubs. Its strict target layout gate currently passes 1 of 27 overlapping checks and fails 26; it is not a final production link. The independently checked portions include 384 floppy BDS body bytes and 2,500 hard-disk BDS bytes. Real SYSINIT ordering and external definitions remain unresolved. `DosDataSg` now uses the target on-disk initial word; its original source spelling and provenance remain unknown. See [initializer evidence](../etc/file-review-009-dosdatasg-initializer.json). See [gate evidence](../etc/file-review-009-target-gate-evidence.json) and [split-source probe](../etc/file-review-009-source-splitter.json).

The following dated or historical notes record earlier investigations; references to a file being active reflect their original context, not current status.

Latest structural evidence: unique exact 24-byte anchors place 6.22 BIOS-derived regions in the early part of 7.0a IO.SYS and kernel-derived regions near 0x11000â€“0x1BFFF. A candidate kernel header begins at 0x113C0, with version bytes 07 00 and a contiguous common header/data run; [evidence](../etc/io-kernel-header-evidence.json), [mapping](../etc/io-bios-kernel-anchors.json). These anchors do not certify complete routines.

File 1 `bios/apmequ.inc` has an initial review: its baseline users are conditional on POWER, which is disabled in the verified 6.22 build, and both POWER objects are 26 bytes. The tested literal APM call patterns do not occur in the target; that is insufficient to rule out other encodings or establish target source identity. It has passed the exact-reference screen; its 7.0a target disposition remains pending and unchanged; [review](../etc/file-review-001-apmequ-initial.json).


An isolated version-constant probe compiled the 6.22 kernel with 7.00 in a disposable source copy. Its `dos/mstable.asm` version field matches IO.SYS 0x113C9, followed by 178 matching error-table bytes; ten bytes in the 6.22 kernel output changed overall. This does not establish the complete 7.0a kernel. [Probe evidence](../etc/version-probe-evidence.json). Additional exact spans associated with reference symbols in four DOS source files were checked directly against the target; [mapping](../etc/kernel-source-anchors.json). No project source was modified.


The frozen floppy boot sector reads four consecutive IO.SYS sectors to physical 0x700, checks `MZ` and the `BJ` marker at file offset 0x200, then jumps to 0070:0200. Thus the separately recorded MZ CS:IP is not the floppy boot entry. [Reproducible static evidence](../etc/boot-entry-evidence.json). The entry's memory/RPL check contains a 40-byte exact match to `bios/msload.asm` Relocate, followed by different relocation instructions; [anchor](../etc/loader-relocate-anchor.json). No source is marked 7.0a-complete based solely on the match.


The one-pass historical reference screening covered all 220 files in ledger order: 190 have at least one exact historical reference (6.0: 188, Astro: 189, 5.0: 87, OpenNT: 19; categories overlap); 30 have no exact match under the explicit mapped paths. The scan found zero textual NT/BOP marker leads; this is not proof that no host-specific dependency exists. Exact reference files skip repeated reference-text audit, while all 220 remain pending 7.0a target disposition and must be addressed in fixed order as binary evidence requires. See [reference screen](../etc/reference-screen.json).


File 9 `bios/msbio1.asm` is active: its fourth drive descriptor now initializes the final far pointer as FFFF:FFFF, as in the 7.0a image. The rebuilt 100-byte descriptor exactly matches the target at IO.SYS 0x51B4; the build changed only the expected two baseline output bytes. The rest of the file remains under review, and the complete IO.SYS still differs. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-msbio1-bds4.json).


File 9 also initializes 25 hard-drive descriptor links to FFFFFFFF. In the preceding build, all 2,500 rebuilt bytes of that array equalled the target IO.SYS range at 0x5218; only the expected 50 bytes changed versus the preceding build. The source file remains active for its other sections. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-msbio1-bdss.json).


A trial moved the 25 hard-drive descriptors immediately after the floppy descriptors, matching 2,891 of 2,900 target bytes. Subsequent comparison located the target CON device header at 0x616 and BDS array at 0x5088, with different relative placement and initial pointer segments compared with the baseline. The trial therefore did not establish the correct memory-segment placement and was reverted; verified FFFFFFFF descriptor initializations remain. A fresh build exactly matches the previously validated descriptor-initialization build. [Correction](../etc/file-review-009-segment-correction.json).


Static far-pointer tracing supports a file-offset mapping `(segment << 4) + offset - 0x100` for all links in the target's 13-entry device chain and four-entry floppy BDS chain. The device chain includes a 7.0a CONFIG$ header between LPT3 and COM2. File 9 now contains the target-derived device header and entry wrapper under DOS7_CONFIG. MASM assembly and a temporary stub-assisted link verified chain order, device attributes, and the wrapper call; the 13-entry config_table and read and init handlers are now implemented, and four contiguous data fields are now source-defined; three initialization dependencies and complete placement remain unresolved, so this is not a complete 7.0a build. See [CONFIG$ source evidence](../etc/file-review-009-config-header.json). [Chain evidence](../etc/device-chains-7a.json).


File 9 CONFIG$ progress: the conditional table and read handler have been added. A temporary stub-assisted link verified the 13 dispatch categories; the 32-byte read code matches the target except for six bytes belonging to three unresolved data-address operands. The normal BIOS build remains identical to the prior build. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-config-table.json).


File 9 CONFIG$ initialization branch is now reconstructed under DOS7_CONFIG. A stub-assisted historical link matched all opcode and branch bytes against the 25-byte target procedure; four address operands remain unresolved. The target requires immediate PUSH, so .286 is enabled locally. The normal baseline build remains byte-identical to its preceding result. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-config-init.json).


File 9 CONFIG$ flags, count, value and XMS-pointer data fields are now defined in source. Their linked relative offsets and nine initialized bytes match the target; the read routine references those definitions. Their exact names and semantics are inferred. Three init dependencies and whole-file placement remain open. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-config-data.json).


File 9 CONFIG$ SYSINIT allocation callback and its BIOS code-segment pointer are now source-defined. The target SYSINIT base is 0x4D30 by segment-variable cross-check; its callback is at 0xEE3E. A stub-assisted link matches all non-address bytes in the 36-byte routine, and default BIOS output remains unchanged. Data layout and runtime pointer initialization remain open. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-config-sysinit.json).


File 9 CONFIG$ callback now uses the existing SYSINIT `alloclim` symbol instead of the provisional external name. The target field is identified within a 32-byte matching data anchor; a historical link with only the remaining dependencies stubbed verified that both reads/writes resolve to the actual `sysinit1.asm` symbol. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-config-alloclim.json).


File 9 defines the two SYSINIT CONFIG$ buffer words that were previously external. The callback resolves both source symbols at the target-observed relative offsets; initial bytes match. Final SYSINIT placement and the SI_start entry remain unverified; the target configuration still requires config_init_flags and the runtime segment-pointer assignment. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-config-buffer.json).


File 9 now includes the target-derived CONFIG buffer validation routine under DOS7_CONFIG. In a stub-assisted link, its 102-byte body matches the target except for address fixups and two semantically equivalent instructions encoded differently by the available MASM. The target-exact encodings, symbol placement and call integration remain unresolved. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-config-restore.json).


ML 6.00 in 16-bit MASM compatibility mode generates the two target immediate instruction encodings without embedded bytes. A diagnostic historical link of the ML-assembled msbio1 module matches 82 of 102 CONFIG validation bytes; the 20 differences are ten unresolved address operands. The temporary ML source copy omitted incompatible IF2/ERRNZ assertions, then the linked offsets were independently checked (orig13=00B4, off_d0=00D0). This is not yet a production build configuration. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-ml6-encoding.json).


The ML CONFIG diagnostic is now reproducible with `extras/tools/build_config_ml_probe.py`. It verifies the source ledger, copied baseline source and object hashes, pinned tools, target hash and the two historical offsets; the experiment links only with explicitly identified test stubs. The current result remains 82/102 target bytes, with 20 address bytes unresolved. [Evidence](../etc/config-ml-repeatable.json).


File 9 now defines the SYSINIT BIOS segment field from `Bios_Data`; its relocated initial word matches target 0070, and CONFIG restore reads that source-defined field. At the time of that experiment the ML diagnostic needed config_init_flags as a test stub; the later no-stub build supersedes that limitation. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-config-biosseg.json).


File 9 now includes the target-derived CONFIG buffer-copy routine. An isolated link matched 69 of 81 target bytes; the other 12 are six layout-dependent address operands. The default BIOS build is unchanged and the reproducible ML diagnostic still verifies the existing restore routine. Calling integration and final segment placement remain open. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-config-copy.json).


File 9 now includes the target-derived XMS initialization routine between the allocation callback and copy routine. The repeatable ML diagnostic verifies the target order and lengths of all four consecutive routines (36, 173, 81, 102 bytes); XMS matches 141/173 bytes, with 16 address operands unresolved. Default BIOS output remains unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-config-xms.json).


File 9 now reconstructs the 7.0 BIOS header near INT 2F jump and source-defined flags word. At that stage both MASM and ML diagnostic BIOS links succeeded without test stubs; the subsequent A20 recovery introduced two explicit unresolved message-related dependencies. The linked flag offset 0008 and initial bytes DC 10 match the target. The four consecutive CONFIG routines still match only 320/392 bytes due to unresolved final addresses; this is not a complete 7.0a IO.SYS. [Flag evidence](../etc/file-review-009-config-flags.json); [no-stub ML diagnostic](../etc/config-ml-no-stubs.json).


File 9 now exports `config_init_segment` so the later initializer can write the pointer used by CONFIG$. Target writer and reader use the same BIOS_CODE offset, and the no-stub ML diagnostic verifies the exported word is initially zero as in the target. The actual writer and full layout remain pending. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-config-pointer.json).


Target near-call candidate scan identifies CONFIG XMS initialization at call site 0x6637, buffer copy at 0x6650, and restore at 0xF137. These remain raw candidate references until instruction boundaries and source ownership are established; they are not connected to the 6.22-based callers yet. [Evidence](../etc/config-call-candidates.json).

The CONFIG SYSINIT segment pointer is now exported from file 9, with initial value and target reader/writer references verified. The actual initialization-module write remains pending. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-config-pointer.json).


In the DOS7_CONFIG branch, AltAH now sits immediately before the Ctrl-Break handler, as in the target; its initial byte and store/IRET instruction pattern match apart from the layout-dependent address. The no-stub ML diagnostic verifies orig13/off_d0 offsets and the existing CONFIG routines. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-altah.json).


File 9 DOS7_CONFIG device common-entry control flow now follows the target request-segment comparison and conditional A20 call. An un-stubbed MASM link matches 17/21 bytes in the target entry; the four remaining bytes are two address operands. The default BIOS build remains unchanged and the no-stub ML CONFIG diagnostic passes. A20 helper internals remain unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-cdev-entry.json).


File 9 now has the 7.0 A20 check, XMS enable/disable and fatal display path. Its 118-byte target region matches in 109 bytes in a temporary stub-assisted MASM link; nine differing bytes are addresses. At that stage the source required `a20_message_ptr` and `a20_retry_vector`; the message pointer has since been defined, while the callback was subsequently reconstructed as recorded below. Diagnostic placeholders are not implementation. The normal baseline build is unchanged and ML CONFIG regression still passes with the two stubs. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-a20.json).


File 9 now defines and exports the A20 error-message pointer. The target's A20 failure path reads BIOS offset 017C, and initialization includes a candidate write to that offset before copying a dollar-terminated message. The linked source field has the target zero initial word; its runtime writer remains unconnected. The callback vector was subsequently defined as recorded below. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-a20-msgptr.json).

File 9 now defines the A20 message callback and its far vector in source. A diagnostic link matches 44/56 target bytes; the remaining 12 bytes are six message-resource address operands. The vector points to the restored callback. Four message-resource symbols remain temporary diagnostic stubs, and the runtime writer for the message pointer remains unconnected. The default reference BIOS build is unchanged. This does not establish a complete 7.0a build. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-a20-report.json); [ML diagnostic](../etc/config-ml-after-a20-report.json).

The A20 pointer writer is now located in the target initialization path after the COMPAQ INT 13 patch and INT 15h/4101h check, corresponding to `bios/msinit.asm` near `configdone`. The target selects the A20 hardware error string at file offset 0x10285 and copies it to DI, storing DI at BIOS-data offset 017C. The writer remains to be reconstructed when that later file is reached; segment state and destination bounds still need verification. [Reproducible evidence](../etc/a20-initializer-evidence.json).

File 9 now restores the DOS 7 `call_orig13` path, including the target interrupt masking and unconditional A20 check after the BIOS call. A temporary-stub ML diagnostic link matches 24/30 bytes at target IO.SYS offset 0x0B10; six bytes are two data addresses and one call displacement. The ordinary reference BIOS build remains unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-call-orig13.json).


File 9 includes a target-derived INT 16h entry, identified by target initialization at 0x0F7A installing BIOS offset 042Dh into interrupt vector table offset 0058h (16h * 4). A diagnostic link matches 32/39 bytes; seven address/displacement bytes differ. The check callback is stubbed in this diagnostic, and old-vector initialization and the counter lifecycle remain pending. An initial INT 13 attribution was corrected before recording this result; the existing block13 path is retained. The default reference BIOS build is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-int16.json).

File 9 now has a DOS 7 INT 15h path identified by the target vector installation at 0x0F70. It matches 47/55 target bytes in the temporary-stub diagnostic; the remaining eight bytes are two near-call displacements and two far-pointer addresses. The target includes a short jump over its AL=1 path, which is preserved without assigning an unverified purpose. Two callback implementations and final layout remain unresolved. The default reference BIOS build is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-int15.json).

File 9 now implements the INT 15h reset callback and the shared DOS 7 VDISK-header clearing routine in BIOS_CODE. In the temporary-stub link they match 3/4 and 37/38 target bytes respectively; the remaining bytes are relative-call and BIOS-data-pointer operands. `int15_reset` is no longer stubbed. Default reference BIOS output remains unchanged; full target layout and remaining callbacks are unresolved. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-vdisk.json).

File 9 DOS 7 `RomVectors` now contains the nine target entries, including 4Fh, 08h, 0Fh and 16h. The 45 linked initial bytes match IO.SYS at 0x0700 exactly, and the linked `NUMROMVECTORS` equals 9. New names avoid an existing SYSINIT symbol collision. The default reference BIOS build remains unchanged. Table initialization and the reboot callback are still pending. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-romvectors.json).

File 9 now reconstructs the DOS 7 INT 19h entry, BIOS_CODE reboot callback and 14-entry hardware-vector table. The diagnostic matches 6/10, 109/112 and 70/70 target bytes, respectively; differences are layout-dependent operands. The table is in BIOS_CODE as in the target, but the 6.22 SYSINIT objects still assume BIOS_DATA when writing it; that integration must be addressed in the later file. Default BIOS output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-int19.json).

File 9 now implements the INT 15h escape and INT 16h check callbacks and their timer fields. Diagnostic byte matches are 15/18 and 60/75; the 10-byte timer-state region matches exactly. The callbacks now share one explicit `keyboard_service` diagnostic stub instead of two stubbed callbacks. Final layout and the shared service remain unresolved; default BIOS output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-keyboard.json).

File 9 now reconstructs the DOS 7 INT 29h A20 entry and full BIOS_CODE character output, including its direct text-screen path. A diagnostic link matches 5/8 and 120/121 target bytes; the remaining bytes are address operands. Default reference BIOS output remains unchanged. Final placement, interrupt installation and the complete target are still unresolved. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-outchr.json).


Correction: target initialization installs offset 03EEh as INT 13h, so the formerly attributed INT 2Fh sequence at file offset 0x09EE belongs to `block13`. The source now checks A20 there and forwards to `i13x`; the true INT 2Fh forwarder at 0x0A6E only jumps to `bcode_i2f`. The BIOS_CODE entry table is ordered as the target 11-entry table and `seg_reinit` handles 11 pointers. `int1a_code` is a diagnostic stub and the INT 13 callback still uses a baseline object. Default BIOS output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-vector-correction.json).

File 9 now groups four saved far pointers and the A20 message pointer immediately after the 11 BIOS_CODE vectors. Their 18 initial bytes and relative offsets match target; the separate 10-byte keyboard timer state remains exact. The first two pointer names are neutral because their complete roles are not yet established. Absolute layout and runtime initialization remain pending; default BIOS output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-vector-data.json).

File 9 now contains the target-derived video entry, its relocated far pointer, and a clock-related entry. The entry byte matches are 8/13 and 4/8; the far pointer points to the restored entry with segment 0070h. The second BIOS_CODE vector at target 0144h is a video callback, not the 6.22 time_to_ticks routine: the source now separates video_service and the legacy ttticks dependency, both still diagnostic stubs. The earlier vector-table attribution is superseded. Default BIOS output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-video-entry.json).

File 9 now implements the target video callback, including its text-mode scrolling path. The diagnostic matches 167/169 target bytes; the remaining two are the displacement to the temporary keyboard_service stub. video_service itself is no longer stubbed. Default BIOS output remains unchanged; the shared service and target-wide layout remain incomplete. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-video-service.json).

File 9 now implements the keyboard service, its 512-byte stack and video-state save/restore helpers. Diagnostic matches are 149/169, 26/26 and 28/28 bytes. keyboard_service is no longer stubbed; its vector setup helper remains a diagnostic stub, and runtime integration and final layout remain incomplete. Default BIOS output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-keyboard-service.json).

File 9 now implements keyboard_vector_setup and its two vector descriptors. The diagnostic matches 91/95 target bytes; the four differences are descriptor-table and INT 15h patch addresses. Both descriptors resolve to their intended source symbols. keyboard_vector_setup is no longer stubbed, while int08_entry remains a diagnostic stub. Default BIOS output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-vector-setup.json).

File 9 now implements INT 08h palette rotation and its two hardware port helpers. The 249-byte target region matches 225 bytes; the remaining 24 bytes are address operands, and the six palette-state initial bytes match. INT 08h is no longer a diagnostic stub. Default BIOS output is unchanged. Installation and self-modifying runtime behavior remain unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-int08.json).

File 9 now implements the keyboard vector installation routine using the existing target-derived descriptor table. The diagnostic matches 99/103 target bytes; the remaining bytes are descriptor-table and INT 15h patch addresses. The runtime caller remains unconnected and default BIOS output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-keyboard-install.json).

File 9 now exports the palette and INT 16h limit fields required by the target INT 2Fh path. Target calls at 0x3EC3, 0x3ED0 and 0x3ED6 reach keyboard_vector_install, keyboard_vector_setup and keyboard_service; the related handler is in the later `bios/msint13.asm` source. The six palette initial bytes and exported field layout are verified. That later source is not yet reconstructed or connected; default BIOS output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-keyboard-exports.json).

Correction and progress: target initialization installs the 0x0A76 entry as INT 6Ch (vector offset 01B0h). File 9 now names it int6c_entry and implements int6c_code; diagnostics match 4/8 and 13/17 target bytes. Earlier int1a_code naming is superseded. Date/time reader helpers remain diagnostic stubs; default BIOS output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-int6c.json).

File 9 now reconstructs the DOS 7 character device dispatcher and code-segment opening layout. The 156-byte region matches 145 target bytes; BC_RETF=00C0h, BC_CMDERR=00C1h, OFF_D0=00D0h and BIOS_DATA_WORD=00DAh, and the following 70-byte vector table is exact. Three device-state symbols remain diagnostic stubs for later sources. The default BIOS output remains unchanged; subsequent code placement and full outputs remain incomplete. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-chardev.json).

File 9 BIOS_CODE routines are now ordered as in the target through the A20 message callback. A contiguous 1773-byte diagnostic region matches 1726 bytes; all 12 measured routine offsets match, nine of those routines match fully, and 47 total bytes remain different. Baseline objects and temporary stubs still participate, so this does not establish a complete 7.0a build. Default BIOS output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-code-order.json).

File 9 now places the DOS 7 BIOS_CODE vector table immediately after CONFIG$: CDEV=0140h and saved-pointer fields occupy 016Ch through 017Ch, matching target absolute offsets. The continuous 1773-byte BIOS_CODE region now matches 1730 bytes (previously 1726); 43 remain different. Default BIOS output remains unchanged. Other BIOS_DATA layout, message resources and complete target builds remain unresolved. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-data-order.json).

File 9 now places tracktable at BIOS offset 017Eh and strategy at 027Ah; their 252 and 11 bytes respectively match the target exactly. ROMVECTORS=0100h and CDEV=0140h remain intact. A separate attempted CLOCK/disk entry move was reverted because it displaced those fixed offsets; coordinated device-chain restructuring remains pending. Default BIOS output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-tracktable-placement.json).

File 9 DOS 7 device chain and CLOCK/disk entries now match their target offsets together: ORIG13=00B4h, CLOCK=00EEh, DSK_ENTRY=00F3h, START_BDS=00F8h, ROMVECTORS=0100h, CDEV=0140h and TRACKTABLE=017Eh. INT08_STUB=02DEh, INT08_ENTRY=02E3h and INT10_ENTRY=03E1h; the complete 249-byte INT 08h implementation is byte-identical. The 234-byte device chain matches 226 bytes; two table offsets and BDS pointer remain different. This supersedes the earlier reverted isolated CLOCK/disk move. Default BIOS output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-chain-int08.json).

File 9 DOS 7 interrupt entries, A20 code and call_orig13 now occupy BIOS offsets 03EEh–052Dh and match all 320 target bytes. XMS=000Eh, I13_DS=050Eh and the intervening interrupt state offsets also match. The larger early BIOS range matches 1257/1304 bytes; 47 bytes remain different due to provisional layout and external dependencies. Default reference BIOS output remains unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-a20-order.json).

File 9 now includes the target MS marker at BIOS_CODE offset 0030h. The 1789-byte region through the A20 report callback matches 1766 bytes in the current diagnostic; 23 address bytes remain different in device dispatch and message-resource references. The default BIOS output is unchanged. The hard-disk patch following the early interrupt range corresponds to later bios/msbio2.asm and has not been modified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-bcode-marker.json).

File 9 CONFIG state fields now occupy target BIOS offsets 052Eh–0537h; their nine initial bytes match, and CONFIG_READ now matches all 32 target bytes. In the reproducible diagnostic CONFIG_RESTORE improved from 82/102 to 88/102, while other CONFIG routines still have unresolved addresses. Default reference BIOS output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-config-state-placement.json).

File 9 now places its four DOS 7 floppy BDS descriptors immediately before the hard-disk BDS array, matching target ordering. The first three descriptors match 96/100 bytes each (their far links differ), the fourth matches 100/100, and the adjacent 2500-byte hard array matches exactly. Current diagnostic far links resolve within its BIOS group, but target segment/offset layout and runtime relocation remain unresolved. Default reference BIOS output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-bds-group.json).

File 9 DOS 7 BDS descriptors and the 2500-byte hard array now reside together in SYSINITSEG, as indicated by target SYSINIT jump and BDS pointers using segment 04E3h. The diagnostic chain resolves within its SYSINIT segment; first three descriptors match 97/100 bytes and the fourth and hard array match completely. The target first descriptor is at segment offset 0358h while the provisional link has 0000h; later initialization content and runtime integration remain pending. This supersedes the prior BIOS-group BDS placement. Default reference BIOS output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-bds-sysinit.json).

The target SYSINIT prefix before BDS spans 0358h bytes and includes interrupt stack-switching code related to later bios/sysinit1.asm: a 32-byte target anchor exactly matches the baseline stack code, and ten interrupt-sharing signatures occur in the prefix. This is static ancestry evidence, not a complete source or linker-order proof. The current BDS placement remains provisional; no padding has been inserted to force the offset. [Evidence](../etc/sysinit-bds-prefix-evidence.json).

File 9 CONFIG BIOS segment field is now an explicit external SYSINIT dependency rather than incorrectly colocated with the CONFIG buffer fields.
