Diagnostic-only stub value 0070h is explicit; its final owning variable name and integration remain unresolved. The 320-byte interrupt region remains exact and the default BIOS build unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-bios-segment-owner.json).

File 9 now imports config_init_segment instead of defining it adjacent to CONFIG_INIT. Target BIOS_CODE offset 2B4Eh is shared by disk initialization and CONFIG_INIT and is written during SYSINIT; its actual source owner remains to be established. The temporary diagnostic stub is explicit. The previously aligned 320-byte interrupt region and default reference build remain unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-init-segment-owner.json).

The reproducible ML diagnostic now enforces 15 exact target regions totaling 4062 bytes and verifies all four BDS link pointers and descriptor bodies. The latest run passes these checks and reports CONFIG_RESTORE 88/102. These are selected-region checks using baseline objects and temporary stubs, not a complete 7.0a build or proof of runtime behavior. [Evidence](../etc/config-ml-with-exact-regressions.json).

File 9 DOS 7 character dispatch now references the original start_sec_h symbol in BIOS_CODE rather than a separate invented disk_start_high symbol. Target character and disk paths both access BIOS_CODE offset 0DF0h. The diagnostic uses an explicit start_sec_h stub; the 6.22 disk objects still use old segment assumptions and require later reconstruction. All 15 selected exact regions pass and the default BIOS build is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-start-sector-unification.json).

File 9 DOS7 character dispatch now uses original auxnum and printdev symbol names, with code-segment word declarations matching target character-driver references. These remain diagnostic stubs until later mschar reconstruction; the earlier char_auxnum/char_printdev names are superseded. All 15 exact-region checks pass, and default BIOS output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-char-state-unification.json).

## M1 T1 S1 Packet

| Field | Required record |
| --- | --- |
| Identifier Mode | New |
| Admission And Approval | Admitted by the user's instruction to reconstruct 7.0a using a full 6.22-based source tree. |
| Objective | Map executable layout and binary anchors to an ordered file-level reconstruction plan. |
| Non-goals | Do not claim byte-identical target outputs until directly compared; no VM boot test. |
| Reference Baseline | Verified full v6.22 source snapshot; image and hashes in target-baseline.json. |
| Candidate Proposal | Start with the MZ header/entry point and unambiguous 6.22 code anchors. |
| Files And ABI Surface | Initially analysis and metadata; modify a source file only on sufficient binary evidence and ledger update. |
| Applicable Rules | extras/docs/rules, user constraints, Microsoft source style. |
| Verification | Validate offsets against complete target bytes and record evidence; build and compare after source changes. |
| Expected Markers | MZ layout, anchor mapping, ordered file disposition. |
| Asset Needs | Image-derived targets, historical build tools, v6.22 baseline. |
| Reporting Requirements | Report number modified and unchanged, and distinguish pending from verified source files. |
| Stop Conditions | Inadequate evidence for a particular source edit; do not invent source to force a match. |
| Exit Criteria | Explain target structure sufficiently to select the first source file and actionable change. |
| Original Owner Request | Reconstruct v7.0a as a full source tree from v6.22, matching three image files. |
| Similar-Issue Sweep | Inventory other affected areas exposed by the structural analysis and record in the ledger. |

Target IO.SYS contains a 39-byte BIOS entry-table relocation routine at file offset 691Dh. It walks BIOS offsets 0140h through 016Bh using four-byte entries, conditionally skipping one entry; unlike the provisional DOS7 seg_reinit, it preserves DI and ES and returns near. This establishes that the 6.22-style routine is not the identified target implementation. Its caller, conditional flag, and source ownership remain unresolved, so the existing sysinit1 far-call dependency is retained provisionally until that file is reconstructed. [Evidence](../etc/target-bios-vector-relocation.json); reproducible with extras/tools/trace_bios_vector_relocation.py.

File 9 now calls read_real_date and read_real_time using the DOS 6 msinit.asm names instead of invented int6c-prefixed names. Target IO.SYS helpers at 27B0h and 286Ah follow the respective reference routines, with target-specific differences still to reconstruct when that source file is reached. Both remain explicit diagnostic stubs. All 15 exact-region checks pass and the reference BIOS build remains unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-real-clock-names.json).

File 9 now places the DOS 7 daycnt2 date accumulator at BIOS_DATA offset 0501h, replacing the earlier neutral clock_update_flag name. Three target read_real_date writes identify this location, and the diagnostic now asserts the symbol offset; all 15 exact regions pass and the reference BIOS build is unchanged. Target bin_date_time and month_table are at BIOS_CODE offsets 074Ch and 0750h; their legacy placement remains provisional pending ownership and module ordering. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-daycnt2-placement.json).

The ML diagnostic now verifies all 1766 already-matching bytes in the 1789-byte initial BIOS_CODE region and explicitly excludes 23 unresolved external table, state and message operand bytes. The check passes alongside the 15 exact-region checks and daycnt2 offset check. This diagnostic still links baseline objects and temporary stubs and is not a complete target build. [Evidence](../etc/config-ml-code-region-regression.json).

File 9 removes unreachable DOS7_CONFIG branches nested inside the legacy IFNDEF DOS7_CONFIG BIOS vector table. The active DOS 7 table is unchanged. The diagnostic target checks pass and the reference BIOS output hash remains unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/config-ml-vector-dead-branches.json).

File 9 removes contradictory DOS7_CONFIG branches inside five legacy-only routines: outchr, block13, call_orig13, int19 and int15. The active DOS 7 implementations are unchanged. The diagnostic target checks pass and the reference BIOS output hash is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/config-ml-legacy-branches-fixed.json).

File 9 no longer includes the unreferenced legacy IsA20Off and EraseVDiskHead implementations in the DOS 7 configuration. The target-derived A20 and dos7_erase_vdisk routines remain in place; legacy routines remain for the reference configuration. The target diagnostic checks pass, and the reference BIOS output hash is unchanged. This removes redundant code but does not establish final BIOS_DATA layout. [Evidence](../etc/config-ml-dead-helpers-fixed.json).

Static target table check identifies wait_count at BIOS_CODE 0730h, month_tab at 0740h, bin_date_time at 074Ch, month_table at 0750h, and the disk error mapping at 0E1Eh. Their initialized bytes match the legacy definitions, but the current BIOS_DATA placements and the owning source modules remain unresolved. The five target data regions are checked by extras/tools/trace_bios_code_data_tables.py. [Evidence](../etc/target-bios-code-data-tables.json).

The target early BIOS data region continues through offset 053Fh; the following target bytes at 0540h begin a disk interceptor related to ibm_disk_io in later bios/msbio2.asm. The diagnostic now verifies the 18 initial bytes at 052Eh-053Fh, in addition to the existing checks. The precise ownership of the zero-valued bytes and final segment contribution order remain unproven. [Evidence](../etc/target-bios-disk-code-boundary.json).

File 9 now uses the original EraseVDiskHead name for the target-derived DOS 7 VDISK clearing routine and both callers, replacing the invented dos7_erase_vdisk name. Its 38 target bytes remain exact in the diagnostic; the reference BIOS output hash is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/config-ml-vdisk-name-restored.json).

Target BIOS vector relocation routine at 691Dh is called at 5E8Bh and 666Eh; the second call follows a BIOS copy and passes ES in AX, matching the reference MovBIOS role. File 9 no longer emits the incompatible DOS 6-style seg_reinit in the DOS 7 configuration. The unmodified reference sysinit1 object still requires that symbol, so the diagnostic supplies an explicit far-return stub; the real target initialization routine and its caller remain to be reconstructed. Target diagnostic checks pass; the reference BIOS output hash is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/target-bios-vector-relocation.json).

Correction: the callback previously named a20_report selects messages for divide overflow, direct disk access, and system halt; target BIOS offset 04F3h dispatches through the vector at 0164h. File 9 now uses descriptive bios_error_report, bios_error_vector and bios_error_dispatch names instead of implying an A20 retry. These names are reconstructed, not original-symbol evidence. Diagnostic target checks pass and reference output remains unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/target-bios-error-messages.json).

File 9 exports far labels bios_error_entry at BIOS 04C2h and bios_error_dispatch at 04F3h. The target initializes a 0070:04C2 pointer at file offset 5FECh and writes offset 04F3h at 144E5h. The diagnostic asserts both offsets; target-region checks pass and the reference BIOS output is unchanged. Caller integration remains pending. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-error-entry-exports.json).

File 9 no longer defines auxbuf in BIOS_DATA for DOS 7: target character code computes auxnum plus BIOS_CODE offset 073Ah, and the reference mschar module uses auxbuf as its indexed character buffer. The reference configuration retains its original definition. The diagnostic explicitly stubs auxbuf pending reconstruction of the later character module; target-region checks pass and the reference BIOS output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-auxbuf-owner.json).

File 9 now omits DOS 7 BIOS_DATA definitions for wait_count, base_century, base_year, month_tab, bin_date_time and month_table because their target uses are in BIOS_CODE. The diagnostic provides explicit named stubs pending later character/clock module reconstruction; target initial table locations are recorded separately. Reference configuration retains its original definitions, target-region checks pass, and reference BIOS output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/config-ml-clock-data-owner-fixed.json), [target tables](../etc/target-bios-code-data-tables.json).

File 9 omits the DOS 7 BIOS_DATA definitions of errin, lsterr, errout, numerr and the FAT/volume labels: target disk code uses corresponding BIOS_CODE tables at 0DF9h-0E38h. The diagnostic explicitly supplies these symbols until the later disk module is reconstructed; target checks pass and the reference BIOS output remains unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-disk-tables-owner.json).

File 9 omits the legacy BIOS_DATA sm92 definition for DOS 7: target default BPB bytes are at BIOS_CODE offset 0E17h and target code references that offset. The diagnostic supplies an explicit sm92 stub pending disk module reconstruction; target checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-sm92-owner.json).

File 9 omits the DOS 7 BIOS_DATA accesscount definition: target media-check code increments, compares to five, and decrements the BIOS_CODE byte at 0DD7h, matching the reference use. The diagnostic provides an explicit stub until disk module reconstruction; target checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-accesscount-owner.json).

File 9 omits the DOS 7 BIOS_DATA tim_drv definition: target media-change logic resets the BIOS_CODE byte at 0DD8h to FFh, matching the reference tim_drv path. Diagnostic uses an explicit stub pending disk reconstruction; target checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-tim-drv-owner.json).

File 9 omits DOS 7 BIOS_DATA definitions for medbyt, wrtverify/rflag, seccnt, spsav and xfer_seg. The target disk read/I/O sequence accesses the corresponding BIOS_CODE offsets 0DD9h, 0DDAh, 0DDCh, 0DECh and 179Fh. Diagnostic stubs preserve baseline object linkage until the later disk module is reconstructed; target-region checks pass and the reference BIOS output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-disk-io-state-owner.json).

File 9 omits DOS 7 BIOS_DATA definitions of dpt, cursec, curhd, curtrk, temp_h and saved_word. Target CHS conversion uses BIOS_CODE offsets 0DE4h, 0DE8h, 0DE9h, 0DEAh, 0DEEh and 0DF2h respectively. Diagnostic stubs retain baseline object linkage until disk reconstruction; target checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-chs-state-owner.json).

File 9 omits DOS 7 BIOS_DATA definitions for motorstartup, settlecurrent, settleslow, save_eot and eot: target setup/restore code accesses BIOS_CODE offsets 0DDEh, 0DDFh, 0DE0h, 0DE2h and 0DE3h. Target setup exchanges the EOT byte, unlike the reference store/read sequence; later disk reconstruction must preserve that behavior. Diagnostic uses explicit stubs; target checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-dpt-timing-owner.json).

File 9 omits DOS 7 BIOS_DATA definitions for save_head_sttl, formt_eot, hdnum, trknum and gap_patch. Target retry and formatting instructions use BIOS_CODE offsets 0DE1h, 179Ah, 179Bh, 179Ch and 179Eh. Diagnostic stubs remain pending disk and IOCTL reconstruction; target checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-format-state-owner.json).

File 9 omits the DOS 7 BIOS_DATA set_id_flag definition. Target code sets BIOS_CODE 0DF8h to 1, calls the BPB path, compares it with 2 and clears it, matching reference set_id_flag behavior. Diagnostic uses an explicit stub pending disk reconstruction; target checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-set-id-owner.json).

File 9 exports reconstructed single_drive_mask=04h and setowner_mask=08h for the DOS 7 BIOS status word at offset 0008h. Target checksingle tests these bits at the points where the DOS 6 reference checks standalone single and fsetowner variables. Their old definitions remain provisional for baseline object linkage; later disk/IOCTL source must be adapted to packed bits. Diagnostic checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-drive-state-masks.json).

File 9 now exports descriptive a20_mask=01h and changeline_mask=02h alongside the existing packed drive-state masks; its two target-derived A20 flag tests use a20_mask. Target BPB code tests bit 02h at the reference fhave96 changeline decision. Diagnostic target checks pass and reference output is unchanged; later driver adaptation remains pending. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-bios-flag-masks.json).

File 9 exports reconstructed k09_mask=10h for the BIOS status word at 0008h. Target nondestructive keyboard read tests that bit before the 0400h request-status test and INT 15h/4100h, matching the reference fhavek09 path. Later character code must use the packed flag; diagnostic checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-k09-mask.json).

A trial removing the legacy packed-drive-flag compatibility fields and dsktnum exposed an alignment-generated 90h at target BIOS offset 053Eh (target 00h). The trial was fully reverted; source SHA256 equals the prior validated k09-mask version and diagnostic checks pass. True bytes and module ordering at this boundary remain unresolved; no padding was added. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-packed-flags-layout-trial.json).

Target INT13 code accesses prevoper at BIOS_CODE 2468h and number_of_sec at 246Ah. A trial removing their provisional BIOS_DATA definitions changed the unproven early BIOS layout and failed the post-interrupt target-byte check, so it was reverted. The prior source hash and diagnostic checks are restored; target ownership remains recorded for later INT13 integration. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-int13-state-ownership.json).

The reproducible diagnostic now checks all 13 CONFIG$ command table entries against linked local symbols, validates their relative positions against target BIOS_CODE offsets 2D40h-2D7Bh, and byte-compares the 32-byte CONFIG_READ path. The diagnostic temporarily exports local labels only in its disposable source copy. Absolute target placement, external pointers and full builds remain unresolved. [Evidence](../etc/config-ml-table-relations.json).

File 9 omits DOS 7 BIOS_DATA definitions for mediatype, media_set_for_format and had_format_error. Target formatting code accesses BIOS_CODE offsets 17A1h, 17A2h and 17A3h. These are explicit diagnostic stubs pending disk IOCTL reconstruction; target checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-format-flags-owner.json).

File 9 omits the DOS 7 BIOS_DATA tempdpt definition. Target format code saves the old DPT far pointer at BIOS_CODE 1796h and the target initial value is FFFFFFFFh, matching the reference role. The diagnostic uses an explicit stub until IOCTL reconstruction; target checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-tempdpt-owner.json).

Correction: target BIOS offsets 000Ch and 000Dh are model_byte and secondary_model_byte, not an inHMA flag and spare byte. Target initialization writes ROM configuration bytes there, and INT13/CMOS paths test them. File 9 now defines the original model symbols at those offsets for DOS 7; the legacy sysinit1 inHMA reference is an explicit diagnostic stub. The diagnostic asserts both offsets; target checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-machine-model-placement.json).

Correction: target BIOS_DATA offset 0069h is keyrd_func and 00B2h is keysts_func; initialization writes 10h/11h and character code reads them. Target step_drv is BIOS_CODE 0DD6h, not BIOS_DATA 0069h. File 9 now defines keyboard fields at their target offsets and removes the incorrect DOS 7 step_drv definition; the diagnostic stubs step_drv until disk reconstruction. Target checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-keyboard-functions-placement.json).

File 9 removes unreachable legacy branches nested inside the DOS 7 vector-table condition; diagnostic and reference builds pass. Separately, the target initial BIOS word at 0003h contains bytes 46h 9Eh while the diagnostic initializes it to zero; target character dispatch reads this location. Its provenance remains unresolved and no invented value was inserted. [Diagnostic](../etc/config-ml-vector-condition-cleanup.json), [header evidence](../etc/file-review-009-dosdatasg-initial-value.json).

Independent installation-media comparison: WIN95/PRECOPY1.CAB/winboot.sys from the local Windows 95 OEM RTM ISO is exactly byte-identical to the 223148-byte target IO.SYS, including BIOS bytes 46h 9Eh at file offset 0603h. The CAB reader reports unsupported multivolume handling, but the captured member is accepted only after full-length and byte-equality checks. This establishes presence in the installation member, not the origin of the word. [Evidence](../etc/target-winboot-installation-comparison.json).

Target initialization at file offset 1502h rounds DI to a paragraph, adds BIOS segment 0070h, and writes the result to BIOS offset 0003h. This corresponds to the reference msinit DosDataSg calculation. The diagnostic now asserts the field offset; its on-disk initial-byte mismatch remains unresolved. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-dosdatasg-runtime-write.json).

File 9 now defines the DOS 7 BDS count and 26-entry dskdrvs table directly after the SYSINIT BDS arrays. Target initialization increments the word at 0EACh and fills pointers from 0EAEh; target count plus table initial 54 bytes match the diagnostic. The reference configuration retains its original initialized BIOS_DATA table and its output is unchanged. Absolute SYSINIT placement, target initializer and later disk-driver segment assumptions remain unresolved. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-dskdrvs-sysinit.json).

File 9 renames config_init_segment to the descriptive sysinit_seg: target BIOS_CODE 2B4Eh is written with CS by SYSINIT, used by the CONFIG$ far call, and loaded as ES for dskdrvs. The name is reconstructed and its final defining source is still unknown; diagnostic uses a stub. Target checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-sysinit-seg-unified.json).

Target dskdrvs ends at SYSINIT offset 0EE2h, while CONFIG buffer fields are accessed at 0A0A0h, 0A0A2h and 0A0A4h. In the current diagnostic those fields immediately follow dskdrvs; their apparent zero-byte match does not prove correct placement. The intervening section layout and ownership remain unresolved. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-sysinit-table-boundaries.json).

File 9 omits the DOS 7 BIOS_DATA_INIT nul_vid and tmp_vid definitions. Target BIOS_CODE offsets 2800h and 280Ch contain the adjacent initial strings, and target copy code uses them as the null and temporary volume-label buffers. Diagnostic uses explicit stubs pending later volume-label reconstruction; target checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-volume-buffers-owner.json).

Correction: target BIOS initialization writes offset 013Fh into hdrv_pat, and BIOS_DATA:013Fh starts at 80h. File 9 now defines harddrv at that target location rather than treating it as an anonymous byte and defining a second copy in BIOS_DATA_INIT. Diagnostic asserts the offset and passes target checks; reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-harddrv-placement.json).

File 9 uses descriptive int19_init_mask=0100h in the BIOS status word and no longer defines the legacy int19sem byte for DOS 7. Target SYSINIT sets the bit and INT19 checks it before hardware vector restoration; reference sysinit1's old byte is an explicit diagnostic stub. Target checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-int19-init-mask.json).

File 9 omits the DOS 7 BIOS_DATA sectorspertrack definition. Target IOCTL code writes and reads BIOS_CODE 17A4h, whose initial word is 36. The diagnostic uses a stub pending disk IOCTL reconstruction; target checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-sectorspertrack-owner.json).

File 9 uses reconstructed BIOS status masks 0200h and 0800h for target multitrack and format operation state, removing the DOS 7 legacy standalone definitions. Target retry counters are in BIOS_CODE at 0DF4h and 0DF6h rather than BIOS_DATA; their old definitions are also omitted. Reference-object compatibility uses explicit diagnostic stubs. Target checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-multitrack-retry-owner.json).

File 9 omits the DOS 7 BIOS_DATA ec35_flag definition. Target INT13 code checks BIOS_CODE 246Bh for any compatible drives and tests its per-drive bit, matching the reference path. Diagnostic uses an explicit stub pending INT13 reconstruction; target checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-ec35-owner.json).

Correction: target BIOS_DATA 0501h is shared by the resident day count and RTC date initialization, rather than a separate daycnt2-only temporary. File 9 now exports daycnt and daycnt2 as aliases at 0501h and removes the duplicate DOS 7 daycnt definition. Target CMOS and rollover decisions use status masks 0020h and 0080h; descriptive mask constants replace the old standalone DOS 7 fields, which remain diagnostic stubs for baseline objects. Target checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-daycnt-unified.json).

File 9 exports int16_tick and int16_count at BIOS offsets 0503h and 0505h. Target clock read increments 0505h, while INT16 checking increments and resets the same word; the final timing rationale is not yet established. Diagnostic asserts both offsets and target checks pass; reference BIOS output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-clock-keyboard-counter.json).

File 9 omits the DOS 7 BIOS_DATA bintobcd and daycnttoday far pointer table. Target clock code uses direct near calls to the BCD conversion at 0DBFh and day conversion at 0D01h; reference objects use explicit diagnostic pointer stubs until later clock reconstruction. Target checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-clock-helper-pointers.json).

File 9 omits the legacy int6c_ret_addr field for DOS7_CONFIG. The target INT 6Ch handler calls its update routine and returns with RETF 2; the reference handler instead stores a return address in the field. The legacy object uses an explicit diagnostic stub. The evidence is limited to this observed path; the complete DOS 7 build remains pending. Target diagnostic checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-int6c-return-owner.json).

The CONFIG diagnostic now reports the linked SYSINIT offsets of bds_count, dskdrvs and the three CONFIG buffer fields alongside their target offsets. All five currently differ in the diagnostic; the 54-byte matching BDS table is a content check and does not establish final segment-relative placement. Diagnostic checks pass; [layout report](../etc/config-ml-sysinit-layout-report.json).

Correction: target BIOS initialization at IO.SYS 1522h far-jumps to 04E3:0EE8 (file 5C18h), whose near jump reaches file 5D30h. This entry is distinct from the SYSINIT BDS start at offset 0358h. The DOS7 branch no longer incorrectly labels the BDS start SI_start; target entry implementation and final placement remain open. Diagnostic checks pass and the reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-sysinit-entry-correction.json).

File 9 restores the DOS 7 SI_start near jump to the SYSINIT move_myself path. The target jump reaches SYSINIT offset 1000h, whose entry matches the reference move_myself sequence. The diagnostic links to a marked stub: its displacement and SYSINIT placement are not target-exact, and six target bytes between the drive table and entry remain unidentified. Diagnostic checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-sysinit-entry-jump.json).

File 9 excludes the legacy hardnum padding, dsktnum and nextspeed bytes from DOS7_CONFIG: the target BIOS offset 0540h begins the disk I/O interception code corresponding to msbio2 ibm_disk_io. The diagnostic retains an explicit dsktnum stub for the unchanged reference object. This does not establish final placement of disk-state variables or the handler. Diagnostic checks pass and reference output remains unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-biosdata-end-correction.json).

File 9 excludes the legacy disksector buffer and feb29 field from DOS7_CONFIG. The preceding diagnostic placed disksector at BIOS 0541h, inside the target disk interception code beginning at 0540h. After removal, feb29 would occupy that code region too. Both are explicit reference-object compatibility stubs, not target implementations. Their actual target placement remains unknown. Diagnostic checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-disksector-feb29-owner.json).

File 9 names and exports the DOS 7 disksector_ptr at BIOS offset 00FCh. Target readboot loads DS:BX from this pointer, and SYSINIT writes DI:ES to it after loading the BIOS data segment. The target initial four bytes and linked offset match. Buffer allocation and later callers remain unreconstructed; diagnostic and reference builds pass. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-disksector-pointer.json).

Target SYSINIT relocation determines the disksector_ptr dynamically: it starts DI at zero, copies the variable-length SYSINIT region with REP MOVSW, enters the relocated code at offset 1080h, saves the resulting DI, and stores that DI with the relocated CS as the buffer far pointer. Thus the buffer begins after the relocated region, not inside the old fixed BIOS_DATA array. Buffer capacity and full relocation remain unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-disksector-relocation-flow.json).

A trial removing the remaining provisional zeroseg, disk-state and flag definitions failed the target BIOS_DATA byte check and was reverted. This diagnostic layout dependency does not establish their target semantics; the final contribution and padding remain unresolved. [Trial](../etc/file-review-009-final-flags-trial.json).

File 9 now removes the provisional DOS 7 copies of prevoper, number_of_sec, fhave96, single, fhavek09 and fsetowner, because prior target evidence places the first two in BIOS_CODE and represents the flags in the status word. Legacy diagnostic objects use explicit stubs. The diagnostic reports two unresolved BIOS tail differences at 0539h and 053Ah instead of treating the entire range as exact; the earlier 18-byte match is superseded. Other target checks pass and the reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-legacy-flags-excluded.json).

File 9 excludes the DOS 6 re_init far-return from its provisional DOS7 BIOS_DATA location; target BIOS 0539h is zero, while the old entry emitted CBh there. A compatibility stub serves the reference objects. The diagnostic BIOS tail now differs at 0539h only, where the legacy Win386 structure currently begins; target ownership of that structure and any re-init entry remain unproven. Diagnostic checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-reinit-owner.json).

File 9 excludes the old Win386 startup/instance structure and V86 focus helper from their provisional DOS7 BIOS_DATA location. Target disk code tests BIOS status bit 40h and inlines its focus call. Compatibility stubs support unchanged legacy objects. The following provisional HMA fields now produce six unresolved tail-byte differences at 0539h-053Eh; removal does not prove the target has no equivalent Win386 data elsewhere. Diagnostic checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-win386-owner.json).

File 9 excludes provisional DOS7 BIOS_DATA definitions for FreeHMAPtr, MoveDOSIntoHMA and SysinitPresent from the target-zero tail region. Reference objects use explicit diagnostic stubs. Diagnostic tail bytes happen to match after removal, but are supplied by stubs; this is not proof of final placement or absence of equivalent HMA state. Other target checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-hma-state-owner.json).

File 9 removes the provisional DOS7 zeroseg field: both target checksingle paths zero BX and load ES from it, where the reference uses zeroseg. Old objects receive a diagnostic-only stub. The exact data check now ends at 0536h; offsets 0537h-053Fh remain unproven despite coincidental diagnostic byte matches. Other checks pass and reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-zeroseg-owner.json).

Target initialization sets DI=0540h as its dynamic BIOS placement boundary and uses the same offset as the optional hard-disk patch source. The linked diagnostic endfloppy label is at 0537h, nine bytes early. The bytes and ownership before the target boundary are unresolved; no filler has been added. The diagnostic now reports this offset mismatch explicitly. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-endfloppy-boundary.json).

Correction to the endfloppy boundary analysis: target dynamic start 0540h is not proven to be the endfloppy symbol. The reference link places endfloppy at 08FEh, BData_end at 0900h, end96tpi at 0918h and ibm_disk_io at 12DEh; BIOS data segments are word-aligned. The nine-byte difference is therefore a cross-layout observation, not evidence that msbio1 needs nine bytes of padding. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-endfloppy-boundary.json).

The CONFIG diagnostic now asserts all four source-defined BIOS offsets 052Eh, 052Fh, 0531h and 0533h. Independent target instruction anchors show flag read and count/value/XMS-pointer writes to those same addresses. Full CONFIG routine placement and semantics remain open. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-config-data-offsets-verified.json).

File 9 removes a redundant nested DOS7_CONFIG test and its unreachable clock_update_flag alternative. The complete stub-assisted diagnostic binary is byte-identical before and after; the DOS 6 reference output also remains unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-condition-cleanup.json).

The diagnostic now checks all 518 bytes between the matched video-state save and restore routines: the NOP, keyboard stack, saved SP/SS and depth initial byte match the target, as does their relative placement. This is a source-defined region, not a compatibility stub; full keyboard integration remains unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-keyboard-save-stack.json).

The diagnostic now asserts all five keyboard timing/state offsets 0503h through 050Bh. Target initial values match the source (0, 0, 200, 200, 0); target code copies the second threshold to the first and decrements the last word in the INT 16h path. These observations do not establish complete runtime semantics. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-keyboard-state-offsets.json).

The CONFIG restore diagnostic now verifies that all 88 non-address bytes match, and independently checks the seven two-byte operands against their linked source symbols and observed target offsets. The 14 address bytes still differ because SYSINIT placement is incomplete; this is not a byte-identical routine. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-config-restore-operands.json).

File 9 renames config_bios_segment to bios_data_segment: target SYSINIT 0F3Eh is read by both disk buffer initialization and CONFIG restore. The new name is descriptive, not a recovered original name; the definition remains an explicit diagnostic stub. The diagnostic binary is unchanged byte-for-byte and the DOS 6 reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-bios-data-segment-name.json).

Target SYSINIT offsets 0142h and 0165h contain unique 24-byte instruction anchors from the reference sysinit1 hardware-stack path before BDS1 at 0358h. The current reference link orders msbio1 before sysinit1, so simply inserting a zero gap cannot restore the target prefix. Source ownership and segment/link arrangement must be resolved when integrating the later SYSINIT code. No later file was modified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-sysinit-stack-before-bds.json).

The four CONFIG routines now have symbol-specific operand checks: across 392 bytes, all 348 non-address bytes match and all 22 layout-dependent word operands resolve to the intended diagnostic symbols and observed target offsets. Their final addresses remain unresolved, and this does not certify complete target byte identity. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-config-all-operands.json).

File 9 combines 25 adjacent DOS7-excluded legacy disk-data conditionals into a single block without changing the declaration order. The diagnostic binary is byte-identical before and after and the DOS 6 reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-legacy-data-block.json).

The diagnostic now asserts all four fdrive symbols point six bytes into their own 100-byte BDS structures. Target SYSINIT derives BDS+6 and stores it in the drive table, confirming the same relationship. This does not establish absolute SYSINIT placement or restore the target initializer. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-floppy-bpb-symbols.json).

Target SYSINIT word A0A6h immediately follows the three CONFIG buffer fields and is used to save and restore an MCB size around a later system-file execution path. The intervening target data also includes IO.SYS and WINBOOT.SYS path strings before config_init_entry at A10Eh. Their original source ownership is not established; this region is not unexplained zero padding. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-config-adjacent-mcb.json).

MSDOS.SYS component: extras/tools/build_msdos_config.py constructs the target six-byte configuration text from its ASCII line and CR LF; two runs produced a full byte match and SHA-256 d28c3a421236bb61081f706193f51f32b9f8b957598d94b6b3b143ae52b1ea03. The output is under ignored extras/build. This does not build IO.SYS or COMMAND.COM and does not complete the project. [Evidence](../etc/msdos-config-build.json).

The BIOS error-report diagnostic now verifies all six source-symbol operands and target resource-table offsets, including the three target relative string offsets 003Fh, 00A8h and 0141h. Resource definitions remain stubs and final placement is unresolved. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-bios-error-operands.json).

File 9 renames the shared BIOS offset 0008h state word from config_init_flags to bios_flags to reflect its observed A20, disk, keyboard, INT 19h and CONFIG uses. The name is descriptive rather than recovered original. The diagnostic asserts its offset, its binary is byte-identical before and after the rename, and the DOS 6 reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-bios-flags-name.json).

File 9 adds descriptive Win386, keyboard-hook and CONFIG allocation masks for the shared BIOS status word. Its existing keyboard and CONFIG instructions now use those constants; target instruction anchors confirm the bit values. The Win386 mask is exported for later integration. Diagnostic bytes and DOS 6 reference output remain unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-shared-status-masks.json).

MSDOS.SYS generation now supports --no-verify, which builds from the source text without reading the target. A test explicitly prohibited target reads in that mode; a subsequent normal run verified full byte identity against the frozen target. [Evidence](../etc/msdos-config-build.json).

File 9 exports multrk_configured_mask=0400h: target MULTITRACK parsing sets this bit and conditionally sets the 0200h enable bit; later initialization sets 0200h by default only when 0400h is clear. The name is reconstructed. Diagnostic bytes and DOS 6 reference output are unchanged; later parser and initialization work remains. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-multitrack-configured-mask.json).

The INT 6Ch diagnostic now verifies its 13 non-address bytes and binds the two near calls to the read_real_date and read_real_time symbols, with target destinations 27B0h and 286Ah. Those diagnostics remain stubs, and complete helper behavior and placement are still pending in later source. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-int6c-call-binding.json).

The device entry diagnostic now verifies all six formerly masked 16-bit operands against their actual linked symbols and target offsets. auxnum, printdev and start_sec_h are still stubs, while con_table and dsktbl come from reference objects; target placement and later implementations remain unresolved. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-device-entry-operands.json).

File 9 documents the observed register inputs and data flow of config_read, config_init_entry and config_restore, using reconstructed comments rather than claiming original text. The diagnostic binary is unchanged and the DOS 6 reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-config-procedure-comments.json).

The current diagnostic and unchanged file 10 reference identify 12 direct msbio2 dependencies still supplied by stubs. The handoff records their source lines and hashes; the target interfaces must be recovered when file 10 is processed. This is not a claim that file 9 or target integration is complete. [Handoff](../etc/file-review-009-msbio2-handoff.json).

File 9 now defines saved_mcb_size immediately after config_buffer_count, matching the target-adjacent SYSINIT word used by the later system-file execution path; initial bytes match. Its name and original source-file ownership are inferred, its diagnostic absolute offset still differs, and the target consumer is not yet reconstructed. Other target checks pass and the DOS 6 reference output remains unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-saved-mcb-size.json).

Target SYSINIT A100h-A10Dh is a 14-byte EXEC0-compatible parameter block immediately preceding config_init_entry at A10Eh. The target sets its environment and command-line segments and uses ES:BX for INT 21h/4B00h on WINBOOT.SYS and IO.SYS. This identifies actual intervening target structure; original ownership and the command data remain unresolved. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-sysinit-exec0.json).

Target EXEC0 command data at A0A8h consists of a 44-byte payload (FFh, count 7, seven six-byte length/offset/segment records), followed by CR. Target execution code writes all seven segment fields before EXEC: six receive SYSINIT CS and the second receives SYSINIT word 0F40h. Record meanings and source ownership remain unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-sysinit-exec-records.json).

The seven EXEC records now have static target destinations mapped: six use SYSINIT CS, one uses the BIOS_CODE segment word. The first points to a 64-byte buffer initially containing a drive-letter slot followed by :\WINDOWS. The remaining pointed data have been recorded without assigning unverified names; relocation behavior is still pending. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-sysinit-exec-record-targets.json).

File 9 now reconstructs the target EXEC command-tail records, their adjacent eight-byte local data, and IO.SYS/WINBOOT.SYS path strings. The diagnostic verifies 33 non-pointer tail bytes, all seven pointer-symbol bindings, the eight initial data bytes and both path strings. Three pointer targets remain explicit stubs; original source ownership, absolute addresses, EXEC0 and the execution path remain unresolved. DOS 6 reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-exec-records-source.json).

File 9 adds the 16-byte empty EXEC environment and 14-byte EXEC0 parameter block after the system paths. The entire adjacent data span through config_init_entry is 118 bytes: all 102 non-pointer bytes match and eight word pointers are symbol-bound but remain layout-dependent. The environment alignment is reported explicitly; three pointer targets remain stubs. DOS 6 reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-exec-complete-data.json).

File 9 exports system_exec_env_end at the final word of the 16-byte environment; target execution code uses its corresponding offset A0FEh when deriving the environment segment. This naming change leaves diagnostic and DOS 6 reference binaries unchanged. The diagnostic environment is not paragraph-aligned, so actual segment calculation remains unresolved. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-exec-env-end.json).

File 9 now computes the binary EXEC command-tail length and record count from label differences rather than hard-coding 44 and 7. The generated diagnostic is byte-identical before and after, and the DOS 6 reference output is unchanged. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-exec-tail-counts.json).

A reproducible isolated probe extracts file 9 SYSINIT BDS, entry and late-data/code into three independently assembled objects. The historical linker interleaves two synthetic probe contributions between them in one SYSINIT segment; repeating the probe produced identical objects. This establishes a possible integration mechanism only: the main msbio1 object has not been split, synthetic gaps/stubs are not target code, and the real stack contribution and link order remain unfinished. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-sysinit-split-probe.json).

The SYSINIT split probe now also compiles a disposable main msbio1 with BDS and CONFIG entry references external, links it with the three extracted source objects, reference objects and explicit stubs, and repeats reproducibly. The resulting link resolves start_bds to the split BDS, verifies 384 floppy BDS body bytes, 2500 hard-disk BDS bytes and 102 non-pointer EXEC data bytes against target. Production splitting and real SYSINIT/link layout remain incomplete. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-sysinit-split-probe.json).

A reusable source splitter now generates the msbio1 main module and three SYSINIT source contributions directly from the maintained file, without reading the target. Native mode preserves historical assertions; ML 6 mode removes them only with post-link orig13/off_d0 checks in the diagnostic. The probe links the generated sources with explicit reference objects and stubs and retains the verified target BDS/EXEC data. Real stack/link ordering and production integration are still incomplete. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-009-source-splitter.json).

A strict file-9 target gate now checks 21 target-derived symbols and six byte regions in a supplied linked BIOS, including SYSINIT layout, BDS, EXEC/CONFIG data and INT 6Ch. The current diagnostic fails 26 of 27 overlapping checks; only the initial BDS count/table bytes pass. A one-byte mutation makes that passing check fail. This is not a full IO.SYS acceptance test. [Report](../etc/file-review-009-target-gate.json), [gate evidence](../etc/file-review-009-target-gate-evidence.json).

File 9 source work has been handed off to integration_pending, not certified: its strict diagnostic gate still fails and target DosDataSg initial bytes remain unexplained. Cross-module symbols and actual SYSINIT order are integration dependencies. File 10 bios/msbio2.asm is now the sole active source file; historical exact matching does not establish DOS 7 identity. Files 1–8 passed the historical reference screen only.

File 10 initial binary anchor: the reference diagnostic install_bds procedure has a 59-byte exact prefix at target IO.SYS file offset 4468h. The remainder and overall behavior have not been validated; see [anchor](../etc/file-review-010-install-bds-anchor.json).

File 10 install_bds now accesses eot via CS in the DOS7_CONFIG build, matching the target segment overrides and branch encoding at IO.SYS 44A3h. Both DOS 7 and reference configurations assembled; the EOT address and remaining module behavior await integration. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-010-install-bds-eot.json).

File 10 mediacheck and changed_drv now access tim_drv through CS under DOS7_CONFIG, matching target instructions at 454Ah and 45D0h. DOS 7 and reference assembly succeeded; the reference object is byte-identical to the preceding diagnostic. Final addresses and full module remain unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-010-media-state-access.json).

File 10 check_vid now uses disksector_ptr for the extended boot signature, FAT count and serial number under DOS7_CONFIG; the target instruction spans and assembly encodings agree apart from unresolved pointer relocations. Both configurations assemble and the reference object remains byte-identical. Branches, absolute addresses and complete integration remain unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-010-check-vid-far-pointer.json).

File 10 DOS7_CONFIG swpdsk now reconstructs the target focus, swap broadcast and two-part prompt flow. The assembly procedure spans 117 bytes like target 44AFh–4523h and selected branch encodings agree; two message-table words remain explicit external dependencies. Reference assembly remains byte-identical to the prior object; full linked code is not yet verified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-010-swpdsk-reconstruction.json).

A relocation-aware listing check now verifies all 103 unmasked bytes of the 117-byte DOS 7 swpdsk routine against target; 14 relocation bytes remain excluded. A changed branch byte was detected by the check. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-010-swpdsk-full-byte-check.json).

File 10 volume-label read, transfer, comparison and FAT-check routines now use DOS 7 segment handling. Both configurations assembled and reference object matches the prior diagnostic. The four target spans total 218 bytes; 198 nonrelocated bytes match and 20 relocation bytes remain unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-010-volume-routines.json).

The file 10 media/volume verification now covers 17 procedures: all nonrelocated bytes match their target spans; relocations remain excluded. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-010-media-volume-routines.json). The old ROM-hook section and final object ownership/order have not yet been established.

File 10 legacy ROM-hook reference object spans for ibm_disk_io (302 bytes) and compaq_disk_io (33 bytes) each occur exactly once, byte-for-byte, in target IO.SYS at 0B40h and 0C6Eh respectively. Production placement and entry references remain unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-010-rom-hook-regions.json).

File 10 dsk_init now loads ES from the DOS 7 SYSINIT segment variable before returning the drive table, matching target opcode sequence at 2AB5h apart from unresolved link operands. Both variants assemble and reference object is unchanged; the 2AB5h entry and 4468h install_bds require noncontiguous integration of the source contributions. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-010-dsk-init.json).

File 10 dsk_init can now be generated as a separate assembly contribution from the maintained source. Both generated objects assemble and link in an isolated diagnostic with explicit message/init stubs; dsk_init nonrelocated bytes match target. Target link order and all addresses remain unresolved. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-010-split-link.json).

File 10 source reconstruction pass has been handed off as integration_pending; this is not a target acceptance. File 11 bios/mschar.asm is now the sole active source file. [Handoff](../etc/file-review-010-handoff.json).

File 11 initial anchor: target swpdsk calls con_flush at IO.SYS 24DEh; its 17 bytes match the linked reference diagnostic exactly. This does not verify the rest of mschar.asm. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-011-con-flush-anchor.json).

File 11 DOS7_CONFIG console non-destructive read now tests the K09 bit in bios_flags; both variants assemble and the reference object is unchanged. Five console routines span 191 bytes, with 167 nonrelocated bytes matching target and 24 relocation bytes unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-011-console-routines.json).

File 11 DOS 7 printer paths now use code-segment printer number and retry state, with a word-sized device number. Both variants assemble and the reference object is unchanged. Console/printer checks cover 13 routines; relocated operands and final linkage remain unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-011-console-printer-routines.json).

File 11 serial routines now use DOS 7 code-segment aux state and buffer addressing. Both variants assemble and the reference object is unchanged. Console/printer/serial checks cover 22 routines; relocation operands and final integration remain unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-011-console-printer-serial.json).

File 11 DOS 7 clock read and GetTickCnt rollover flow are reconstructed. Both variants assemble and the reference object remains unchanged; their nonrelocated bytes match target. int6c_update is an unresolved cross-file near symbol, and clock write plus final relocation remain pending. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-011-char-clock-routines.json).

File 11 DOS 7 clock write now validates length and time fields and uses direct near conversion routines. Both variants assemble and reference object remains unchanged. Target and source place time_to_ticks on opposite sides of tim_writ, so that call displacement is masked pending integration, along with relocation operands; bin_to_bcd and daycnt_to_day near exports also remain unresolved. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-011-char-clock-write.json).

File 11 build preparation can reorder time_to_ticks after tim_writ as in target; the assembled near-call displacement 0020h now matches target. This generated source is not yet part of a full production build. The four target device tables have 58 entries, all matching their source handler mappings. Relocated addresses, helper exports and full linkage remain pending. [Clock order](../etc/file-review-011-clock-target-order.json), [tables](../etc/file-review-011-dispatch-tables.json).

File 11 near clock helper references use distinct near-interface names so they cannot silently bind to the legacy far procedures. An isolated diagnostic link succeeds with three explicit RET-only near stubs and emits no-stack/no-entry warnings; actual near helpers and production link remain outstanding. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-011-diagnostic-link.json).

File 11 has entered integration_pending, not target acceptance; file 12 bios/msdioctl.asm is now the sole active file. [Handoff](../etc/file-review-011-handoff.json).

File 12 GetDeviceParameters now propagates target-observed BDS flag 0800h when building the device BPB; its semantic name is not established. DOS 7 and reference MASM configurations assemble and the reference object is unchanged. ML 6 naturally emits the target AND encoding; all 83 nonrelocated bytes of the 91-byte procedure match target, with eight relocation bytes pending. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-012-get-device-parameters-ml6-verified.json).

File 12 SetDeviceParameters now uses the DOS 7 BIOS flag, code-segment media/track state and target DS handling. Both MASM configurations and ML 6 assemble; the reference object remains identical. In the 194-byte target procedure, 174 unmasked bytes match; four differing bytes are the two local call displacements to RestoreOldDpt and SectSizeToSectIndex, whose target placement remains unresolved, and 16 relocation bytes are unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-012-device-parameters.json).

File 12 FormatTrack now uses DOS 7 format-state segment accesses, clears the target-observed BDS high flag, and follows target DS handling. DOS7 and reference variants and ML 6 assemble; reference object unchanged. Its 205-byte target span has 162 matching unmasked bytes, nine differences at local call/jump displacement bytes, and 34 unresolved relocation bytes. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-012-format-track.json).

File 12 VerifyTrack, ReadTrack, WriteTrack and ReadWriteTrack now use DOS 7 packed flags and code-segment state. Both variants and ML 6 assemble, and the reference object remains identical. All 132 unrelocated bytes across these 164 bytes match target; 32 relocation bytes remain pending. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-012-track-verify-readwrite.json).

File 12 TrackIo now uses target BIOS flag and CS-relative disk state. Both variants and ML 6 assemble; the reference object remains identical. TrackIo and two size conversion routines total 159 bytes: 129 nonrelocated bytes match target and 30 relocation bytes remain unverified. SetDeviceParameters now differs in one unmasked byte of the RestoreOldDpt call displacement. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-012-trackio.json).

File 12 SetDasd, SetMediaForFormat and ResetDisk now use DOS 7 CS state and zero-segment setup. All 261 unrelocated bytes across these 297 target bytes match; 36 relocation bytes remain pending. Reconstructing them also resolves all nine earlier unmasked FormatTrack displacement differences. Both configurations and ML 6 assemble; the reference object is identical. [Media evidence](../etc/file-review-012-media-format.json), [format evidence](../etc/file-review-012-format-track-current.json).

File 12 ToRom, drive ownership and RestoreOldDpt paths are reconstructed; five procedures total 211 bytes with 175 nonrelocated bytes matching target and 36 relocation bytes pending. The previously differing SetDeviceParameters local displacement now matches. Both configurations and ML 6 assemble; reference object remains identical. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-012-rom-ownership-dpt.json), [parameter regression](../etc/file-review-012-device-parameters-resolved.json).

File 12 media ID and boot I/O paths now use DOS 7 disk sector far pointer, code-segment state and packed changeline flag. Both variants and ML 6 assemble; reference object remains identical. Four procedures total 228 bytes: 196 unrelocated bytes match and 32 relocation bytes remain unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-012-media-id.json).

File 12 adds target IOCTL minor codes 48h and 49h, two INT 13h paths and their extension check; the names are inferred. Three new routines have 77 matching nonrelocated bytes and six relocation bytes pending. Target read/write tables have 9/10 entries and support-query table has 13 codes; all mapped target handlers agree. Dispatch, access flag and sense procedures have 188 matching nonrelocated bytes with 24 relocation bytes pending. [New code](../etc/file-review-012-int13-ioctl.json), [dispatch](../etc/file-review-012-dispatch-tables.json), [other routines](../etc/file-review-012-dispatch-access-sense.json).

File 12 all 30 procedures were checked together: 1590 nonrelocated bytes match across 1844 bytes; 254 relocation bytes remain unverified. Its ML6 object links in the existing diagnostic with documented stubs and no-stack/no-entry warnings; this is not a target build. [All procedures](../etc/file-review-012-all-procedures.json), [diagnostic link](../etc/file-review-012-diagnostic-link.json).

File 12 source pass is integration_pending, not target acceptance; file 13 bios/msdisk.asm is the sole active file. [Handoff](../etc/file-review-012-handoff.json).

File 13 Media_Chk now uses the DOS 7 changeline bit and CS-relative last-drive state; the reference object remains identical after assembly. SetDrive has 18 matching nonrelocated bytes, Media_Chk has 100 matching and two differing bytes in the local maperror call displacement; 20 relocation bytes across both routines remain unverified. ML 6 assembly currently fails on the baseline dosmac.inc invoke/IF2 macro; no include file was altered. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-013-media-check.json).

File 13 access timing now uses CS-relative accesscount; ML 6 assembled a diagnostic copy using the unchanged TESTB macro and an independently verified FALLTHRU adjacency, without modifying the project include. The 51-byte timing routine has 43 matching nonrelocated bytes and eight relocation bytes unverified. This reduced macro environment is not yet a production build. [Timing](../etc/file-review-013-media-timing-ml6.json), [macro probe](../etc/file-review-013-ml6-macro-probe.json).

File 13 Get_Bpb now uses DOS 7 CS set_id_flag and packed changeline state. Its three entry, request-writeback and ID-clear routines total 119 bytes; 101 nonrelocated bytes match target and 18 relocation bytes remain unverified. Both MASM variants and the reduced-macro ML diagnostic assemble; reference object is unchanged and FALLTHRU adjacency rechecked. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-013-bpb-entry.json).

File 13 GetBP uses DOS 7 boot-validity register, packed changeline state and CS-relative BPB fallback data. Both MASM variants and reduced-macro ML diagnostic assemble; reference object is unchanged. Across the 190-byte target routine, 175 compared bytes match, five differing bytes are local call displacements to not-yet-reconstructed routines, and ten relocation bytes remain unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-013-getbp.json).

File 13 readbootsec now preserves DS, uses the returned DS:BX sector buffer, returns validity in CX, and reproduces the target OEM-signature flag handling. Both MASM variants and reduced-macro ML diagnostic assemble; reference object remains identical. Of 108 bytes, 107 match and one call-displacement byte remains different until read_sector is reconstructed. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-013-entry-through-readboot.json).

File 13 movbpb, mov_media_ids and readfat now use the DOS 7 far sector buffer and preserve DS as observed. Both MASM variants and reduced-macro ML diagnostic assemble; reference object remains identical. Three routines total 133 bytes with 123 nonrelocated bytes matching and 10 relocation bytes pending. The earlier readbootsec local call difference is now resolved; GetBP has two remaining maperror displacement bytes. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-013-bpb-copy-fat.json), [entry regression](../etc/file-review-013-entry-current.json).

File 13 read_sector now obtains its DMA buffer through disksector_ptr and uses CS-relative retry state. Both MASM variants and reduced-macro ML diagnostic assemble; reference object remains identical. In 126 target bytes, 105 compared bytes match; three differ in local again/set_tim call displacements and 18 relocation bytes remain unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-013-read-sector.json).

File 13 disk open/close and write entries now use DOS 7 packed changeline and code-segment write state. Both MASM variants and reduced-macro ML diagnostic assemble; reference object remains identical. Five entry routines total 84 bytes, with 73 matching compared bytes, one differing local call displacement byte, and ten relocation bytes unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-013-disk-entries.json).

File 13 checksingle now uses DOS 7 packed owner/single-drive flags and target zero-segment setup. Both MASM variants and reduced-macro ML diagnostic assemble; reference object remains identical. The 129-byte routine has 119 matching unmasked bytes, two bytes differ because ML 6 emits 3A E0 for cmp ah,al while target uses 38 C4 for the same comparison, and eight relocation bytes remain unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-013-checksingle.json).

File 13 diskio now includes target-observed boot-media write guard, CS state, packed changeline checks and 32-bit bounds branches. The extended disk dispatch is an unresolved near external pending reconstruction. Both MASM variants and reduced-macro ML diagnostic assemble; reference object identical. Of diskio 279 bytes, 231 compared bytes match, two local call-displacement bytes differ, and 46 relocation bytes remain unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-013-diskio-provisional.json).

File 13 iosetup and diddleback now restore target DPT exchange and CS-relative state. Both MASM variants and reduced-macro ML diagnostic assemble; reference object remains identical. Three routines total 129 bytes, with 102 matching compared bytes, one differing set_tim call byte and 26 relocation bytes pending. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-013-io-setup-done.json).

File 13 block now reproduces target DPT EOT handling, packed multitrack state and the 0800h BDS-flag transfer boundary. Both MASM variants and reduced-macro ML diagnostic assemble; reference object remains identical. The 117-byte routine has 105 matching unrelocated bytes and 12 relocation bytes pending. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-013-block.json).

File 13 disk now uses DOS 7 CS state, packed multitrack flag, and target retry selection and BIOS error handling. Both MASM variants and reduced-macro ML diagnostic assemble; reference object unchanged. Of 210 bytes, 175 compared bytes match, three differ in local branch displacements to later routines, and 32 relocation bytes remain unverified. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-013-disk-lowlevel-current.json).

File 13 verify, retry, error and speed-control paths now use target packed flags and CS state. A target retry branch was corrected to retain drive/track updates. Eight routines total 418 bytes: all nonrelocated bytes match except one local call-displacement byte to again; 70 relocation bytes remain unverified. Reference object unchanged; MASM and reduced-macro ML diagnostics assemble. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-013-disk-retry-current.json).

File 13 error mapping now uses the target code-segment error table and a target-observed nine-entry count; set_tim uses CS accesscount. Three routines total 72 bytes, with 62 nonrelocated bytes matching and ten relocation bytes pending. Previously differing local call displacements in Media_Chk, GetBP, read_sector, diskio and dskerr now match. Reference object unchanged; MASM and reduced-macro ML diagnostics assemble. [Error routines](../etc/file-review-013-error-time-retry-current.json), [core regression](../etc/file-review-013-core-regression.json).

File 13 extended INT 13h disk dispatch, packet handling and retry code were reconstructed from the target. The retry branch was corrected to retain AH across retry. MASM assembles DOS7 and reference configurations; the reference object is byte-identical to the prior reference object. ML diagnostic listing comparisons match all 404 non-relocated bytes across diskio and the three extended routines; 82 relocation bytes and complete linked IO.SYS remain unverified. Reproduce with `python extras/tools/verify_msdisk_extended.py extras/build/msdisk-ml-probe-3ctbnq76/diskml.lst --ml-listing --report extras/build/msdisk-extended-report.json`.

File 13 consolidated comparison covers all 38 mapped procedures (2308 bytes): 2002 unrelocated bytes match, 304 relocation bytes remain unverified, and checksingle has two mismatched bytes due to its existing cmp instruction encoding. Four intervening ranges (29 bytes total) are outside the procedure-based check and still need their own automated comparison. [Report](../etc/file-review-013-all-routines.json); the verifier exits nonzero while the two known differences remain.

The four gaps between mapped file 13 routines total 29 bytes and all match their target bytes in the reduced-macro ML listing ([report](../etc/file-review-013-gaps.json)). For `cmp ah,al`, DOS 4 and DOS 6 MASM and ML produced 3A E0, while a standalone NASM probe produced target 38 C4. JWasm produced a 3A E0 listing but returned a general failure. NASM compatibility with the full MASM source, addresses, and the linked target is not established; no production assembler change was made ([probe](../etc/file-review-013-cmp-encoders.json)).

File 13 relocation inspection checks six internal packet addresses against the target using the independently inferred BIOS_Code base 0x1c50; all six match (12 bytes). For 146 external references, repeated identical instruction forms infer consistent target operands, with relative call targets normalized. This consistency check does not verify the external symbol definitions or the final link; those 292 bytes remain unverified. [Report](../etc/file-review-013-relocations.json).

File 13 moved to integration_pending with its encoding and linked relocation issues explicitly recorded. File 14 `bios/msdskpr.inc` matches the 5.0, 6.0 and 6.2 reference files exactly; seven instructions in the target confirm four used structure field offsets and two ROM operation constants. It remains integration_pending because other declarations and consumers remain unverified. File 15 `bios/msend.asm` is active. [File 14 evidence](../etc/file-review-014-msdskpr.json).

File 15 `bios/msend.asm` exactly matches 5.0, 6.0, and 6.2; MASM successfully assembled it to the identical 181-byte reference object. Its empty end-label segments cannot establish target end addresses without the actual module layout, so it is integration_pending. File 16 `bios/msequ.inc` is active. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-015-msend.json).

File 16 `bios/msequ.inc` is byte-identical to 5.0, 6.0, and 6.2. Seven target disk instruction checks cover four constants; other declarations and consumers require integration. It is integration_pending; file 17 `bios/msgroup.inc` is active. [Evidence](../etc/file-review-016-msequ.json).

File 17 `bios/msgroup.inc` matches 5.0, 6.0 and 6.2 exactly; the identical file was used in the successful DOS7-configured msdisk MASM assembly. File 18 `bios/msinit.asm` now reconstructs DOS 7 read_real_date and read_real_time register/segment usage and error flow. Both MASM configurations assembled; the reference object remained identical. File 18 DOS7_CONFIG now orders in_bin, date_verify, time_verify, bcd_verify, daycnt_to_day, bin_to_bcd and bcd_to_bin to match the target, while preserving the original reference configuration. File 18 now uses the target K09 check and INT 6Ch vector installation immediately before the A20 message initialization, without the legacy dynamic-copy path in DOS7_CONFIG.
